ACCESS TO THE BACHELOR STUDY PROGRAM

Studying with the (Fach-) Abitur (general or subject-restricted school-leaving qualification)

Admission requirement: Proof of the general higher education entrance qualification, the subject-restricted higher education entrance qualification, or the (subject-restricted) entrance qualification for universities of applied sciences

On some certificates the restriction is noted: "valid for...". These must strictly be observed by the matriculation department. Here we only accept:

  • "valid for all federal states"

  • "valid for Thuringia"

Addendum

Note: With the addendum "Fulfils the requirements for recognition in all federal states with the exception of Bavaria and Saxony", it is valid for all federal states except for Bavaria and Saxony. Therefore it is also valid for Thuringia and the prospective student can be admitted.

1. Abitur (German school-leaving qualification)

Allgemeine Hochschulreife (general higher education entrance qualification)

The general higher education entrance qualification is uncritical. Here, the certificate in officially certified form in German or English is sufficient (see the section "The Official Certification").

Fachgebundene Hochschulreife (subject-restricted higher education entrance qualification)

The subject-restricted higher education entrance qualification from the German-speaking legal area (Germany, Austria, Switzerland) is uncritical. Here, the certificate in officially certified form in German or English is sufficient (see the section "The Official Certification").

If, however, a subject-restricted higher education entrance qualification from abroad is presented, a trial study period (Probestudium) is required. The required documents must be submitted in translated form, as described under the section "The Official Certification".

PAGE 15 OF 245

2. Fachhochschulreife (entrance qualification for universities of applied sciences)

In the case of an HZB obtained through the (subject-restricted) entrance qualification for universities of applied sciences, the school-based part (in officially certified form) and the vocational part must both equally be proven.

Allgemeine Fachhochschulreife (general entrance qualification for universities of applied sciences)

The general entrance qualification for universities of applied sciences is uncritical. If it is not an overall certificate, the school-based and the vocational part must be submitted. Only the school-based part of the FHR must be submitted as an officially certified copy.

Fachgebundene Fachhochschulreife (subject-restricted entrance qualification for universities of applied sciences)

In the case of the subject-restricted entrance qualification for universities of applied sciences, the focus of the subject-restricted entrance qualification for universities of applied sciences must correspond in subject terms to the intended Bachelor program. If the school-based focus and the intended degree program do not match in subject terms, admission is only possible via the trial study period (Probestudium).

3. Special Features of School-Leaving Qualifications in Baden-Württemberg

General higher education entrance qualification (Abitur) from general Gymnasien, vocational Gymnasien and the Freie Waldorfschule Baden-Württemberg

Certificates of the general higher education entrance qualification that were obtained at general and vocational Gymnasien as well as at the Freie Waldorfschule in Baden-Württemberg are uncritical. The following annotation appears on the Abitur certificate:

"Vereinbarung zur Gestaltung der gymnasialen Oberstufe und der Abiturprüfung" (Agreement on the design of the upper secondary level of the Gymnasium and the Abitur examination) (Resolution of the Kultusministerkonferenz [Standing Conference of the Ministers of Education and Cultural Affairs] of 07.07.1972 in the version of 18.02.2021)

Entrance qualification for universities of applied sciences Baden-Württemberg

General entrance qualification for universities of applied sciences Baden-Württemberg

The general entrance qualification for universities of applied sciences of the upper secondary level of the Gymnasium, or issued by general and vocational Gymnasien in Baden-Württemberg, is uncritical. It can be accepted for direct higher education access to all Bachelor degree programs at the IU under the following conditions:

  1. the school-based and job-related part is confirmed in the annotation on the certificate
  2. the annotations of the Kultusministerium (Ministry of Education and Cultural Affairs) and the resolutions on the upper secondary level of the Gymnasium are included on the certificate:

PAGE 16 OF 245

"Vereinbarung zur Gestaltung der gymnasialen Oberstufe und der Abiturprüfung" (Agreement on the design of the upper secondary level of the Gymnasium and the Abitur examination) (Resolution of the Kultusministerkonferenz of 07.07.1972 in the version of 18.02.2021)

or

"Vereinbarung über den Erwerb der Fachhochschulreife in beruflichen Bildungsgängen" (Agreement on the acquisition of the entrance qualification for universities of applied sciences in vocational education programs) (Resolution of the KMK of 05.06.1998 in the version of 09.03.2001)

Those certificates which certify both the school-based and the job-related part with reference to one of the respective above-mentioned resolutions entitle the holder to study at universities of applied sciences in all federal states in the Federal Republic of Germany, i.e. also in Thuringia. If a corresponding annotation is missing, study applicants are to be referred to the competent authority of the federal state in which she/he obtained the school-leaving qualification. As a rule, this is the school that issued the certificate.

Entrance qualification for universities of applied sciences issued by the Berufskolleg II in Baden-Württemberg

Admission to a study program at the IU – Internationale Hochschule is only possible under certain conditions for study applicants with an entrance qualification for universities of applied sciences that was obtained at a Berufskolleg II in Baden-Württemberg. Admission is possible if, in addition to the school-based qualification and the assistant qualification obtained in parallel to it, in conjunction with either

a) a relevant six-month work placement, i.e. one oriented according to the respective specialization of the school-based training, or

b) at least two years of relevant professional activity, or

c) a completed vocational training of at least two years (outside the educational program leading to the entrance qualification for universities of applied sciences)

is proven. If study applicants possess the above-mentioned qualifications, the Kultusministerium Baden-Württemberg issues, upon application by the study applicants, a confirmation for the purpose of submission for an individual case decision on the determination of equivalence of the study entitlement at universities of applied sciences in a federal state outside Baden-Württemberg.

Without the confirmation of the Kultusministerium Baden-Württemberg, admission to studies at the IU is not possible.

In the interest of the study applicants, inquiries to the Thuringian Ministry must urgently be refrained from. The sole responsibility for certifying the nationwide validity of the FHR from Baden-Württemberg, or for the confirmation for an individual case decision on the determination of equivalence of the study entitlement at universities of applied sciences in a federal state outside Baden-Württemberg, lies exclusively with the Kultusministerium Baden-Württemberg.

PAGE 17 OF 245

Entrance qualification for universities of applied sciences issued by the Freie Waldorfschule in Baden-Württemberg

Admission to a study program at the IU – Internationale Hochschule is only possible under certain conditions for study applicants with an entrance qualification for universities of applied sciences that was obtained at a Freie Waldorfschule in Baden-Württemberg. Admission is possible if the following proofs are provided:

  1. Certificate of the entrance qualification for universities of applied sciences (school-based part and job-related part, issued by the school) from the Freie Waldorfschule Baden-Württemberg with the annotation of the Kultusministerium Baden-Württemberg on the acquisition of the entrance qualification for universities of applied sciences at Freie Waldorfschulen:

"Verordnung des Kultusministeriums Baden-Württemberg über den Erwerb der Fachhochschulreife an Freien Waldorfschulen vom 30.07.2013 (Gesetzblatt für Baden-Württemberg 2013, Seite 258)" (Ordinance of the Kultusministerium Baden-Württemberg on the acquisition of the entrance qualification for universities of applied sciences at Freie Waldorfschulen of 30.07.2013 (Law Gazette for Baden-Württemberg 2013, page 258))

In conjunction with

  1. Proof of an additional vocational-practical activity in the extracurricular area of at least 9 months, issued by the company / institution, from which the duration of the employment, the area of responsibility and the days of absence must be evident.

Or alternatively

2a) Voluntary Social Year (Freiwilliges Soziales Jahr) (duration 1 year) 2b) Military service or alternative military service (duration 1 year) 2c) Federal Voluntary Service (Bundesfreiwilligendienst) (duration 1 year) 2d) Completed vocational training or equivalent professional experience pursuant to § 5 paragraph 1 sentence 1 number 2 1BKFHVO (in the respectively applicable version)

Completed services (see above no. 2) of less than one year, as well as continuous participation in a vocational training program, are credited toward the duration of the practical activity, provided that the regulations applicable to the practical activity are fulfilled.

If study applicants with an entrance qualification for universities of applied sciences from the Freie Waldorfschule in Baden-Württemberg possess the above-mentioned qualifications (nos. 1 and 2), the Kultusministerium Baden-Württemberg issues, upon application by the study applicants, a confirmation for the purpose of submission for an individual case decision on the determination of equivalence of the study entitlement at universities of applied sciences in a federal state outside Baden-Württemberg.

Without the confirmation of the Kultusministerium Baden-Württemberg, admission to studies at the IU is not possible.

In the interest of the study applicants, inquiries to the Thuringian Ministry must urgently be refrained from. The sole responsibility for certifying the nationwide validity of the FHR from Baden-Württemberg, or for the confirmation for an individual case decision on the determination of equivalence of the study entitlement at universities of applied sciences in a federal state outside Baden-Württemberg, lies exclusively with the Kultusministerium Baden-Württemberg.

PAGE 18 OF 245

4. Special Features of School-Leaving Qualifications in Rheinland-Pfalz

General entrance qualification for universities of applied sciences Rheinland-Pfalz

The Ministry in Rheinland-Pfalz is generally responsible for the recognition (Zuerkennung) of the entrance qualification for universities of applied sciences (FHR).

However, a letter of recognition is – by arrangement with the Ministry – not required if one of the following qualifications is available as proof of the practical part:

  • 12 months of work placement in a company
  • 12 months of FSJ (Voluntary Social Year) and Federal Voluntary Service (Bundesfreiwilligendienst)
  • a completed vocational training
  • 18 months of military service

In all other cases, in which applicants have deviating qualifications for the vocational-practical part, the proofs of both the school-based and the vocational-practical part must be examined by the Ministry in Rheinland-Pfalz and a letter of recognition must be applied for:

Contact: thomas.berg@bm.rlp.de

5. Certificate of the Feststellungsprüfung (assessment examination)

Foreign study applicants can enroll for the Feststellungsprüfung at state universities in Germany. The successfully completed Feststellungsprüfung corresponds to the subject-restricted higher education entrance qualification and enables admission to studies. At the IU there is no Feststellungsprüfung, but we accept the passed Feststellungsprüfung from other higher education institutions and universities. The certificate of the Feststellungsprüfung serves as proof of language skills at level C1 if German was an examination subject and it was completed with at least the grade 4.

The certificate of the Feststellungsprüfung is only valid in combination with the underlying school certificates from the country of origin, and they must be submitted together in officially certified form.

PAGE 19 OF 245

6. In the case of discontinued studies at another higher education institution or when changing higher education institution to the IU

In the case of discontinued studies at another higher education institution or when changing higher education institution to the IU, one cannot assume that a higher education entrance qualification (HZB) exists; it must always be examined completely anew, i.e. all documents for establishing the HZB must be available to us and are examined as standard.

Only applicants for whom no admission/matriculation obstacle exists can be accepted. Here, the utmost attention must be paid to the reason for the de-registration (see the specifications on: "Matriculation after compulsory de-registration")!

7. Entrance examinations, trial study programs, internal admission tests of other higher education institutions

The successful passing of an entrance examination (Eingangsprüfung), a trial study program (Probestudium) or other internal admission tests etc. of other higher education institutions, in particular in Germany, does not on its own lead to admission at the IU and cannot be accepted for admission at the IU. The admission requirements at the IU always apply.

PAGE 20 OF 245

Studying without the (Fach-)Abitur (general or subject-restricted school-leaving qualification)

Professionally qualified applicants with direct access

1. Meister title / Meister examination (Meisterprüfung) from Germany and the DACH region

Meister examinations (Meisterprüfungen) (at least DQR level 6) from Germany are placed on an equal footing with the German Abitur and therefore entitle the holder to direct admission to the Bachelor's degree program.

For admission, the officially certified Meister certificate (Meisterurkunde) or the certificate of the Meister examination must be submitted.

Comparable Meister titles from the DACH countries outside Germany (Austria and Switzerland) can be accepted, on the basis of the "Bilaterales Abkommen zur gegenseitigen Anerkennung von Berufsqualifikationen" (Bilateral Agreement on the Mutual Recognition of Professional Qualifications), as equivalent or indeed of higher value in relation to the German Meister title at DQR level 6.

Further information on the European Qualifications Framework (EQR) via: https://europa.eu/europass/de/compare-qualifications

An additional proof of professional recognition in Germany or the certificate of equivalence of the foreign qualification (EU) to a German qualification is not required.

Detailed information on the admission of professionally qualified applicants: see the guide "Bachelor für beruflich Qualifizierte" (Bachelor for professionally qualified applicants)

2. Fachwirt, Techniker and other (advanced vocational) further training programs

Qualifications from advanced vocational further training programs that are assigned, according to the German Qualifications Framework (DQR), at least to level 6 or to a higher-value level are placed on an equal footing with at least the entrance qualification for universities of applied sciences and enable direct admission to the Bachelor's degree program. Proof of professional experience is therefore not needed.

All further training programs from Germany that comprise a scope of greater than/equal to 400 teaching hours and that build upon a regular training occupation can likewise be accepted. Proof of professional experience is therefore not needed.

The subject area plays no role in the choice of the degree program.

Comparable further training programs from DACH countries outside Germany (Austria and Switzerland) can be accepted, on the basis of the "Bilaterales Abkommen zur gegenseitigen Anerkennung von

PAGE 21 OF 245

Berufsqualifikationen" (Bilateral Agreement on the Mutual Recognition of Professional Qualifications), as equivalent or indeed of higher value in relation to the German further training program at a minimum of DQR level 6.

Comparable further training programs from other EU countries and from countries elsewhere abroad can likewise be accepted if the level (EQR) assigned to the further training program from the (European) foreign country is equivalent or of higher value in relation to a German further training program at a minimum of DQR level 6.

Further information on the European Qualifications Framework (EQR) via: https://europa.eu/europass/de/compare-qualifications

Further training programs from countries elsewhere abroad that cannot be assigned to the European Qualifications Framework (EQR) always require an individual case-by-case examination by the Richtlinienmanagement (Guidelines Management).

An additional proof of professional recognition in Germany or the certificate of equivalence of the foreign qualification (EU) to a German qualification is not required.

Detailed information on the admission of professionally qualified applicants: see the guide "Bachelor für beruflich Qualifizierte" (Bachelor for professionally qualified applicants)

3. Equivalent and equated professional further training programs (pursuant to §60 para. 1 sentence 1 no. 3 letters d and e ThürHG)

The following professional further training programs in the learned occupation are, pursuant to the ThürHG (Thuringian Higher Education Act), equivalent to the Meister examination or are placed on an equal footing with the Meister examination, if prior to attendance a vocational training of at least two years was successfully completed and the qualification corresponds to the Rahmenvereinbarung über Fachschulen (Framework Agreement on Fachschulen) (resolution of the Kultusministerkonferenz (Standing Conference of the Ministers of Education and Cultural Affairs) of 7 November 2002 in the respectively valid version (note the annotation on the certificate)):

  • staatlich anerkannte Erzieherin or staatlich anerkannter Erzieher (state-recognized educator),
  • staatlich anerkannte Heilpädagogin or staatlich anerkannter Heilpädagoge (state-recognized curative education specialist),
  • staatlich anerkannte Heilerziehungspflegerin or staatlich anerkannter Heilerziehungspfleger (state-recognized curative education nurse),
  • staatlich anerkannte Motopädin or staatlich anerkannter Motopäde (state-recognized motopedic therapist),
  • staatlich anerkannte Medizinpädagogin or staatlich anerkannter Medizinpädagoge (state-recognized medical educator),
  • staatlich anerkannte Fachkraft für soziale Arbeit (state-recognized specialist for social work),
  • staatlich anerkannte Augenoptikerin or staatlich anerkannter Augenoptiker (state-recognized optician),
  • staatlich anerkannte Familienpflegerin or staatlich anerkannter Familienpfleger (state-recognized family carer),
  • staatlich anerkannte Wirtschaftsinformatikerin or staatlich anerkannter Wirtschaftsinformatiker (state-recognized business informatics specialist),

PAGE 22 OF 245

  • staatlich geprüfte Logistikerin or staatlich geprüfter Logistiker (state-certified logistics specialist),
  • staatlich geprüfte hauswirtschaftliche Betriebsleiterin or staatlich geprüfter hauswirtschaftlicher Betriebsleiter (state-certified home economics operations manager),
  • staatlich geprüfte Gestalterin or staatlich geprüfter Gestalter (state-certified designer),

As well as the qualification as:

  • Wirtschaftsprüferin or Wirtschaftsprüfer (auditor),
  • Steuerberaterin or Steuerberater (tax advisor),

If after this a relevant professional activity of at least two years was carried out.

As well as:

  • the acquisition of the qualification for a career in the higher intermediate service (gehobener Dienst) or of an equivalent level of education for employees in the public service,

if after this acquisition a relevant professional activity of at least two years was carried out

As well as a further training qualification, provided that the courses comprise at least 400 teaching hours and beforehand a vocational training in a recognized training occupation was successfully completed. These qualifications include in particular:

  • Fachkauffrau or Fachkaufmann (certified commercial specialist),
  • Fachwirtin or Fachwirt (certified specialist administrator),

  • Motopädagogin or Motopädagoge (motopedagogue),

If, before the training at the Verwaltungs- und Wirtschaftsakademie, a recognized vocational training of at least two years was successfully completed.

PAGE 24 OF 245

Professionally qualified applicants without direct access

1. Vocational training and professional experience

We generally enable professionally qualified persons to access studies.

Applicants who have a recognized vocational training of at least two years at a minimum of DQR level 4 as well as a subsequent professional activity of three years are professionally qualified for taking up a Bachelor's degree program. Depending on the subject focus of the vocational training and subsequent professional experience, applicants can be admitted to a subject-corresponding degree program via the trial study program (Probestudium) and to a degree program that does not correspond to the subject by successfully passing the entrance examination (Eingangsprüfung) (see section: Trial study program (Probestudium) or Entrance examination (Eingangsprüfung)).

Professional qualifications at DQR level 3 from Germany: The following recognized training occupations, or training occupations deemed to be recognized, in the dual system with a training duration of at least 2 years, which are assigned to DQR level 3, can be taken into account within the framework of the admission of professionally qualified applicants:

  1. Änderungsschneider/ Änderungsschneiderin (alterations tailor)
  2. Ausbaufacharbeiter/ Ausbaufacharbeiterin (interior construction skilled worker)
    • Specialization: carpentry work, stucco work, tile, slab and mosaic work, screed work, thermal, cold and sound insulation work, drywall construction work
  3. Berg- und Maschinenmann/ Berg- und Maschinenfrau (mining and machinery worker)
    • Specialist field: driving and extraction, transport and maintenance
  4. Chemielaborjungwerker/ Chemielaborjungwerkerin (junior chemical laboratory worker)
  5. Fachkraft für Automatenservice (specialist for vending machine service)
  6. Fachkraft für Gastronomie (specialist for gastronomy) – Specialization: restaurant service, system gastronomy (until 01.08.2022 Fachkraft Gastgewerber (hospitality specialist))
  7. Fachkraft für Holz- und Bautenschutzarbeiten (specialist for wood and building protection work)
  8. Fachkraft für Kurier-, Express- und Postdienstleistungen (specialist for courier, express and postal services)
  9. Fachkraft für Lederverarbeitung (specialist for leather processing)
  10. Fachkraft für Metalltechnik (specialist for metal technology)
    • Specialist field: assembly technology, construction technology, machining technology, forming and wire technology
  11. Fachkraft Küche (kitchen specialist)
  12. Fachlagerist/ Fachlageristin (warehouse specialist)
  13. Fahrradmonteur/ Fahrradmonteurin (bicycle mechanic)
  14. Hochbaufacharbeiter/ Hochbaufacharbeiterin (building construction skilled worker)
    • Specialization: masonry work, concrete and reinforced concrete work, furnace and chimney construction work
  15. Industrieelektriker/ Industrieelektrikerin (industrial electrician)
    • Specialist field: operating technology, devices and systems

PAGE 25 OF 245

  1. Isolierfacharbeiter/Isolierfacharbeiterin (insulation skilled worker)
  2. Maschinen- und Anlagenführer/ Maschinen- und Anlagenführerin (machine and plant operator)
    • Specialization: metal and plastics technology, textile technology, textile finishing, food technology, print finishing and paper processing
  3. Polster- und Dekorationsnäher/ Polster- und Dekorationsnäherin (upholstery and decoration sewer)
  4. Produktionsfachkraft Chemie (production specialist chemistry)
  5. Produktprüfer Textil/ Produktprüferin Textil (product inspector textiles)
  6. Servicefachkraft für Dialogmarketing (service specialist for dialogue marketing)
  7. Servicefahrer/ Servicefahrerin (service driver)
  8. Servicekraft für Schutz und Sicherheit (service worker for protection and security)
  9. Textil- und Modenäher / Textil- und Modenäherin (textile and fashion sewer)
  10. Tiefbaufacharbeiter/ Tiefbaufacharbeiterin (civil engineering skilled worker)
    • Specialization: road construction work, pipeline construction work, sewer construction work, well construction and special foundation engineering work, track construction work
  11. Verkäufer/ Verkäuferin (salesperson)
  12. Vorpolierer Schmuck- und Kleingeräteherstellung/ Vorpoliererin Schmuck- und Kleingeräteherstellung (pre-polisher in jewelry and small appliance manufacturing)

Professional qualifications from the DACH countries outside Germany (Austria and Switzerland) can be accepted, on the basis of the "Bilaterales Abkommen zur gegenseitigen Anerkennung von Berufsqualifikationen" (Bilateral Agreement on the Mutual Recognition of Professional Qualifications), as equivalent to a German vocational training at a minimum of DQR level 4.

Vocational training programs from other EU countries can likewise be accepted if the level (EQR) assigned to the training from the European foreign country is equivalent to a German vocational training at a minimum of DQR level 4. Initial orientation is provided by the meaningful evidence in the certificate as well as the Diploma Supplement.

Further information via: https://europa.eu/europass/de/compare-qualifications

An additional proof of professional recognition in Germany or the certificate of equivalence of the foreign qualification (EU) to a German qualification is not required.

Detailed information on the admission of professionally qualified applicants: see the guide "Bachelor für beruflich Qualifizierte" (Bachelor for professionally qualified applicants)

PAGE 26 OF 245

PROFESSIONAL EXPERIENCE

The manner and the extent in which this professional experience is recognized are described in the following points:

Full-time employment In principle, 32 h / per week is to be assumed as full-time. Everything below this is part-time and is then counted proportionately. The weekly working hours performed must be evidenced by the applicant. If full-time working hours are stipulated collectively under an employment agreement by a trade union and are described as full-time in the contract, this is to be counted accordingly as full-time. If the described full-time number of hours is below 32 h per week, this is examined by the Head of Richtlinienmanagement (Guidelines Management) for Matriculation and Recognition.

Part-time employment Any employment <32h / per week corresponds to part-time employment. Any number of hours can be taken into account as part-time that is designated in the employment contract as regular part-time. This applies irrespective of the amount of the wage or the payment of wage tax. Part-time employment is counted proportionately according to its extent.

In principle, marginal employment relationships (temporary/casual jobs) and mini-jobs are not taken into account.

Working student activity and internships Working student activities and internships (after completion of the Bachelor's degree) can only be taken into account as professional experience in individual cases and after examination by the Richtlinienmanagement within the framework of a case-by-case decision. Exception: The practical professional year after the 1st Staatsexamen (state examination) in medicine and law (if successfully completed) can, in consultation with the Richtlinienmanagement, be accepted as 1 year of professional experience for the MBA and the continuing education Master's program.

Self-employment Professional self-employment can also be counted as professional experience where the scope of work is at least 32 h per week. For this, the applicant must assure us by affidavit (Eides Statt) that he is engaged in professional self-employment. For this purpose, the form "Eidesstattliche Versicherung über die berufliche Selbständigkeit" (Affidavit concerning professional self-employment) must be completed and submitted by the applicant. A copy of the trade license (Gewerbeschein) must without fail be enclosed with it.

PAGE 27 OF 245

Further training Holders of further training qualifications for which examination regulations exist pursuant to § 53, 54 of the Berufsbildungsgesetz (BBiG) (Vocational Training Act), § 42, 42 a HwO (Crafts Code), provided the courses comprise at least 400 teaching hours, receive a general higher education entrance qualification. The applicant must submit the corresponding documents from which the scope of 400 h is evident.

2. Trial study program (Probestudium)

With subject-corresponding vocational training (subject-restricted HZB).

Professionally qualified applicants who do not have an advancement qualification (Aufstiegsqualifizierung) but who are aiming for a subject-corresponding degree program can be admitted to studies through a successful trial study program (Probestudium).

2.1. Requirements for the trial study program
  • a vocational training of at least two years in a recognized training occupation at a minimum of DQR level 4

and subsequently

  • at least three years of professional experience in full-time

The trial study program is limited to the first two subject semesters. In order to successfully complete the trial study program, a study achievement of at least 15 ECTS credit points must be earned during this time. For part-time students, the maximum duration of the trial study program is extended proportionately. The trial study program can be ended early by successfully passing the entrance examination (Eingangsprüfung). After successful completion of the trial study program, the student receives an HZB for the degree program he/she is aiming for at the IU; the ECTS earned are credited. In the case of an unsuccessful trial study program, de-registration takes place. Re-enrollment in the same degree program is not possible.

2.2. Examples for the trial study program

  • Vocational training as a Metzger (butcher) and professional experience as HZB for BA BWL (Business Administration) (= entrance examination)

  • Vocational training as a Bürokaufmann (office administrator) and professional experience as HZB for BA BWL (= trial study program)

PAGE 28 OF 245

2.3. Recognition in the trial study program

Recognized ECTS do not reduce the ECTS to be achieved in the trial study program, since it is precisely through this minimum threshold that the ability to study must be demonstrated.

2.4. Trial study program in the case of HZB through the subject-restricted Fachhochschulreife

Applicants with a subject-restricted Fachhochschulreife (subject-restricted entrance qualification for universities of applied sciences) must complete the trial study program if a degree program from a subject-foreign area is being aimed for. Here too it is possible to end this early by taking the entrance examination (Eingangsprüfung).

2.5. Subject areas for the trial study program

An overview of the subject areas or vocational training programs which, in the case of professionally qualified applicants (always at least a 2-year vocational training at a minimum of DQR level 4 with a subsequent and building-upon professional experience of at least 3 years in full-time), lead to taking up the trial study program can be found in the ADMISSION HUB of the RLM on SharePoint:

Link: Leitfaden fachaffiner Berufsausbildungen zur Zulassung zum Probestudium (Bachelor) (Guide to subject-affine vocational training programs for admission to the trial study program (Bachelor))

Please note that this is intended as orientation and not all training occupations are listed. If you are unsure or cannot assign a training occupation, please contact the Richtlinienmanagement.

PAGE 29 OF 245

3. Entrance examination (Eingangsprüfung)

For professionally qualified applicants with vocational training that does not correspond to the subject.

Professionally qualified applicants who provide evidence of a vocational training of at least two years in a recognized training occupation at a minimum of DQR level 4 and subsequently at least three years of professional experience in full-time can be admitted to a degree program that does not correspond to the subject by successfully passing the entrance examination (Eingangsprüfung).

The entrance examination has a scope of 10-15 ECTS and consists of 3 courses:

  • academic work (wissenschaftliches Arbeiten)
  • subject course from the degree program aimed for (these are firmly defined for each degree program)
  • study aptitude test (Studierfähigkeitstest)

The period within which the three courses must be taken and successfully passed is limited to 1 year.

The achievements of the entrance examination (with the exception of the study aptitude test) are recognized.

The applicants here are only participants and only become students after passing the entrance examination.

The modules of the entrance examination to be taken are currently laid down in the EPO and will in future be regulated directly in the curriculum.

PAGE 30 OF 245

GENERAL SPECIAL FEATURES OF FOREIGN QUALIFICATIONS

Anabin

If the admission requirements for a degree program at the IU were obtained abroad, the state certification of the educational institution and the equivalence of the qualification can be determined via the database "Anabin". For us, equivalence is deemed to exist if the higher education institution and above all the specific qualification is classified in Anabin with H+. With H-, no admission is possible. With H+/-, the Head of Richtlinienmanagement (Guidelines Management) for Matriculation and Recognition decides whether a trial study program (Probestudium) may possibly be required. If an educational institution is not listed there, consultation must be held with the Head of Richtlinienmanagement for Matriculation and Recognition.

Certificate of equivalence (Äquivalenzbescheinigung)

If a study applicant has completed the qualification qualifying for studies at an educational institution not listed in Anabin, he can have the equivalence with a German/European educational qualification certified by the relevant district government (Bezirksregierung) by means of an equivalence letter. If this is then available in officially certified or original form, the study applicant can be enrolled in the regular way.

This also applies to the completion of a vocational training abroad. This can be presented, for example, by means of a certificate of equivalence from the IHK (Chamber of Industry and Commerce).

Exception: vocational training in Austria and Switzerland. Since this is the German legal sphere (DACH), the prognosis decision within the right of access is fundamentally present. This means that vocational training programs from Austria and Switzerland can be recognized as equivalent.

Foreign applicants must be able to provide evidence of a language level of at least B2. proof of C1 continues to be necessary!

Test Requirement Level
TestDaF TND4 (min. 4 points in all subsections) C1
DSH DSH2 C1
Telc Telc C1 C1
ÖSD ÖSD C1 C1

All other proofs of language proficiency must be examined individually. Here, the scope and the language level must be indicated. The proof of language proficiency must not be older than 5 years.

"Er/Sie hat damit gemäß §1 Abs. 1 und 2 des Bundesgesetzes über die Berufsreifeprüfung, BGBI I Nr. 68/1997 idgF, die mit der Reifeprüfung einer höheren Schule verbundenen Berechtigungen erworben." ("He/She has thereby, pursuant to §1 paras. 1 and 2 of the Bundesgesetz über die Berufsreifeprüfung (Federal Act on the Berufsreifeprüfung), BGBl I No. 68/1997 as amended, acquired the entitlements associated with the Reifeprüfung of a higher school.")

Target group: applicants with school and / or higher education final certificates or study achievements from Ukraine

  • If school certificates or higher education qualifications, due to the war, are only available as simple copies, as scans or photos and cannot be presented in the original or submitted as officially certified copies, then send these cases to the Richtlinienmanagement for a case-by-case decision

BACHELOR ENTRANCE EXAMINATION (BACHELOR ZUGANGSPRÜFUNG)

Applicants with foreign educational qualifications and an indirect / indirectly subject-restricted HZB can be admitted for the degree program aimed for at the IU within the framework of the Bachelor entrance examination (Bachelor Zugangsprüfung). The prerequisite is the evidence and the confirmation of the indirect / indirectly subject-restricted HZB (Feststellungsprüfung (assessment examination)/Studienkolleg (preparatory college)) on Anabin.

The Bachelor entrance examination consists of 2 modules, which are part of the Bachelor's degree program:

  • Academic work (Wissenschaftliches Arbeiten)
  • Subject course from the degree program aimed for (this is firmly defined for each degree program)

The period within which the two courses must be taken and successfully passed is limited to 1 year.

The achievements of the Bachelor entrance examination are recognized.

The applicants here are only participants and only become students after passing the Bachelor entrance examination.

The modules of the Bachelor entrance examination to be taken are currently laid down in the OZBM.

PAGE 36 OF 245

CONVERSION OF FOREIGN GRADES AT THE IU

The conversion of individual grades earned abroad is in principle a matter for the respectively competent examination boards and is carried out on the basis of the attached conversion table. This was created in accordance with the recommendation of the Hochschulrektorenkonferenz (HRK) (German Rectors' Conference) on the basis of the modified Bavarian formula, taking into account many years of empirical experience and country-specific particularities.

Example:

In Great Britain, 100% is theoretically the top grade, which, however, is usually not awarded. In order to ensure a fair conversion, the maximum value to be inserted into the modified Bavarian formula is adjusted downward on the basis of the IU's empirical experience.

For the following countries, deviating maximum values apply: Australia (for grades 100%-50%), Belgium, France, Great Britain, Ireland, Canada (for grades 100%-50%), the Netherlands, Portugal, the Philippines and South Africa. In these countries, the top grade does not correspond to the actually achievable maximum grade.

Further notes on conversion:

  1. The conversion is based only on the national grades ("Local Grades") that are shown on the official Transcript of Records.

  2. Further details alongside the "Local Grade" such as ECTS grade, Grade Point Average, Grade Point, Class Performance Evaluation, or similar are not taken into account in the conversion.

  3. If grades in % and another grading system are shown simultaneously on the Transcript of Records, the % grade counts.

  4. Any conversion recommendations of the foreign higher education institutions likewise cannot be taken into account.

  5. The examination board reserves the right to review the conversion table at regular intervals and to change it if necessary. For the grade conversion, the version of the table valid on the day of the application is decisive.

  6. If students in Europe take part in an Erasmus program, it may be that the host university grades them according to the ECTS grading system (A-B-C-D-E-F). If no other grading system is shown on their Transcript of Records, the conversion is carried out according to ECTS grades.

PAGE 37 OF 245

MATRICULATION AFTER COMPULSORY DE-REGISTRATION IN GERMANY

If a study applicant was previously compulsorily de-registered at another German higher education institution due to violations of the study and examination regulations, he cannot be re-enrolled in a degree program with identical content.

IMPORTANT: The degree awarded (e.g. Bachelor of Science or Bachelor of Arts) plays no role here! It is purely about the content of the degree program! A change from Bachelor of Science to Bachelor of Arts is therefore not possible!

We examine according to the comparability model. Under Thuringian law, an applicant with a definitively failed examination/compulsory de-registration is only not to be admitted if he has already studied unsuccessfully in the same degree program.

That means: We compare the entire content of the curriculum of the previous degree program and of the degree program aimed for, and not only the failed module. The definitively failed module is therefore not decisive, but rather the entire content of the curriculum.

Important here: please do not fixate exclusively on the name of the degree program. It is always about the entire content of the study program. The designation of the degree program is a first indication of a similarity in content. This alone is not sufficient for a decision.

Examples in the case of compulsory de-registration

Example 1: Degree program from which the applicant was compulsorily de-registered: Finanzmanagement (Financial Management). Degree program aimed for with us: Finanzmanagement (Financial Management).

Here it is quite clearly recognizable that the same or similar subject contents are involved. Therefore, a precise examination on the basis of the curriculum and the module handbooks is necessary. Please request all required documents for this (curriculum, Transcript of Records and the module handbooks) and send the case to the Richtlinienmanagement for examination.

Example 2: Degree program from which the applicant was compulsorily de-registered: Finanzierung und Investition (Financing and Investment). Degree program aimed for with us: Finanzmanagement (Financial Management).

Here it can likewise already be established from the designations that the same or similar subject contents are involved. Therefore, a precise examination on the basis of the curriculum and the module handbooks is necessary; although the degree programs do not have exactly the same designation, they could be structured identically in terms of content. Please request all required documents for this (curriculum, Transcript of Records and the module handbooks) and send the case to the Richtlinienmanagement for examination.

PAGE 38 OF 245

Example 3: Degree program from which the applicant was compulsorily de-registered: Finanzmanagement (Financial Management). Degree program aimed for with us: Soziale Arbeit (Social Work).

Here it can already be quite clearly recognized from the designation of the degree programs that a degree program of a different subject is involved. A brief look at the curriculum is therefore sufficient here. A more extensive examination is not necessary and nothing stands in the way of enrollment.

Important in the case of compulsory de-registration

If applicants have already studied at another higher education institution or university in Germany, attention must always be paid to the reason for which they were de-registered!

For this, it is required that a de-registration certificate stating the reason is submitted by the applicants. The de-registration certificate stating the reason must be applied for separately by the applicants at their former higher education institution. Alternatively, the applicants can also submit a certificate of good standing (Unbedenklichkeitsbescheinigung) issued by their former higher education institution.

In case of uncertainties, please always contact the Head of Richtlinienmanagement (Guidelines Management) for Matriculation and Recognition!

Degree program B.A. Kommunikationsdesign (Communication Design)

(re-accredited – study start from 01.10.2024)

For admission to the B.A. Kommunikationsdesign, in addition to proof of the higher education entrance qualification (HZB), the successful taking and passing of the design aptitude examination (gestalterische Eignungsprüfung) is required.

The design aptitude examination must be taken before the start of studies or before the trial studies (Probestudium) or the entrance examination (Eingangsprüfung).

During the design aptitude examination, applicants have the status of participants (Teilnehmer:innen).

Degree program B.Sc. Logopädie (Speech Therapy)

This is a program restricted to a specific target group.

This degree program is aimed only at Logopäd:innen (speech therapists) who have successfully completed their vocational training in Germany.

Admission is only possible if a successfully completed vocational training in Germany as a Logopäd:in is proven!

and a completed vocational training as a Logopäd:in

PAGE 49 OF 245

Degree program B.Eng. Maschinenbau (Mechanical Engineering)

For this degree program, it is required to complete a subject-relevant six-week preliminary internship (30 days) in a subject-specific company before the start of studies.

The internship must be completed in at least three mechanical-engineering-related fields of activity:

  1. Machining (cutting) manufacturing processes: Filing, chiseling, sawing, thread cutting by hand, turning, planing, milling, drilling, countersinking, reaming, broaching, grinding, honing, lapping.

  2. Assembly and testing: Pre-assembly and final assembly in the one-off and series production of machines, vehicles, apparatus and plants in mechanical engineering, or of ships and ship components in shipbuilding; printed circuit board assembly, measuring with mechanical, electrical, pneumatic and optical measuring methods, gauging, surface metrology, special measuring methods in mass production, becoming acquainted with the production-related tolerance ranges as well as the relationship between accuracy and costs.

  3. Primary shaping and forming: Construction and layout drawing of a pattern, assembling the box parts and pattern cores, mold making, hand molding with patterns and templates, becoming acquainted with wet and dry casting, assisting in the core-making shop, in machine molding and in casting [e.g. sand casting, permanent mold casting, die casting, centrifugal casting, shell molding and investment casting]. Sintering, powder metallurgy and plastic injection molding, open-die and closed-die forging, cold forming/extrusion, rolling, deep drawing, metal spinning, stamping, fine blanking, bending, straightening, riveting.

  4. Joining and separating processes: Oxy-acetylene, arc and resistance welding, flame cutting, special processes of welding and cutting, soldering, stamping, adhesive bonding [welding courses can also be recognized].

Instead of the preliminary internship, the required knowledge can be proven through equivalent preliminary internships that were completed for applications to other higher education institutions, equivalent relevant prior knowledge from subject-appropriate vocational training programs, or comparable subject-specific professional activities in the corresponding subject-appropriate training occupations. This also applies to all equivalent evidence from the DACH region or also from other EU countries or from abroad internationally.

Proof of the preliminary internship is provided by:

  • Certificate of the successfully completed relevant internship
  • Vocational training certificate (certified copy)*
  • Employment reference
  • Employer confirmation

Degree program B.Eng. Mechatronik (Mechatronics)

For this degree program, it is required to complete a subject-relevant six-week preliminary internship (30 days) in a subject-specific company before the start of studies.

The internship must be completed in at least three mechatronics-related fields of activity:

  1. Machining (cutting) manufacturing processes: Filing, chiseling, sawing, thread cutting by hand, turning, planing, milling, drilling, countersinking, reaming, broaching, grinding, honing, lapping.

  2. Assembly and testing: Pre-assembly and final assembly in the one-off and series production of machines, vehicles, apparatus and plants in mechanical engineering, or of ships and ship components in shipbuilding; printed circuit board assembly, measuring with mechanical, electrical, pneumatic and optical measuring methods, gauging, surface metrology, special measuring methods in mass production, becoming acquainted with the production-related tolerance ranges as well as the relationship between accuracy and costs.

  3. Technical drawing and technical documentation: Rules and standards in technical drawing, descriptive geometry in technical drawing; legal foundations and standards for technical documentation; planning, execution and systematics of technical documentation; preparing or modifying technical drawings and technical documentation.

  4. Joining and separating processes: Oxy-acetylene, arc and resistance welding, flame cutting, special processes of welding and cutting, soldering, stamping, adhesive bonding [welding courses can also be recognized].

  5. Electrical engineering and information technology: Manufacture of components, parts, assemblies and devices of electrical engineering; assembly, testing, repair and maintenance of apparatus, devices, plants and systems; becoming acquainted with systems of measurement, control, regulation and automation technology; working with as well as programming of information technology systems in all technical areas of the economy.

For this study program it is required to complete, before the start of studies, a subject-relevant six-week preliminary internship (30 days) in a subject-specific company.

The internship must be completed in at least three activity areas related to industrial engineering–electrical engineering:

  1. Linear electrical engineering
  2. Electric fields and alternating current technology
  3. Semiconductor physics
  4. Electrical measurement technology
  5. Digital and information technology
  6. Electrostatic fields
  7. Circuit simulation
  8. Transistors and transistor circuits
  9. Magnetic fields
  10. Electromagnetic waves
  11. Operational amplifiers

Instead of the preliminary internship, the required knowledge can be proven through equivalent preliminary internships that were completed for applications at other higher education institutions, equivalent relevant prior knowledge from subject-appropriate vocational trainings, or comparable subject-specific professional activities in the corresponding subject-appropriate training occupations. This also applies to all equivalent proofs from the DACH region or also from EU or international countries abroad.

Study Program B.Eng. Wirtschaftsingenieurwesen Maschinenbau

For this study program it is required to complete, before the start of studies, a subject-relevant six-week preliminary internship (30 days) in a subject-specific company.

The internship must be completed in at least three activity areas related to industrial engineering–mechanical engineering:

  1. Machining production processes: Filing, chiseling, sawing, thread cutting by hand, turning, planing, milling, drilling, countersinking, reaming, broaching, grinding, honing, lapping.

  2. Assembly and testing: Pre-assembly and final assembly in the individual and series production of machines, vehicles, apparatus and plants in mechanical engineering or of ships and ship parts in shipbuilding; printed circuit board assembly, measuring with mechanical, electrical, pneumatic and optical measuring methods, gauging, surface measurement technology, special measuring methods in mass production, becoming familiar with production-related tolerance sizes as well as the relationship between accuracy and costs.

  3. Primary forming and forming: Construction and layout drawing of a model, assembling the box parts and model cores, mold making, hand molding with models and templates, becoming familiar with wet and dry casting, participation in the core-making shop, in machine molding and in casting [e.g. sand casting, permanent mold casting, die casting, centrifugal casting, shell molding and investment casting]. Sintering, powder metallurgy and plastic injection molding, open-die and closed-die forging, cold forming/extrusion, rolling, deep drawing, spinning, stamping, fine blanking, bending, straightening, riveting.

  4. Joining and separating processes: Oxy-fuel, arc and resistance welding, flame cutting, special processes of welding and separating, soldering, stamping, adhesive bonding [Welding courses can also be recognized].

Instead of the preliminary internship, the required knowledge can be proven through equivalent preliminary internships that were completed for applications at other higher education institutions, equivalent relevant prior knowledge from subject-appropriate vocational trainings, or comparable subject-specific professional activities in the corresponding subject-appropriate training occupations. This also applies to all equivalent proofs from the DACH region or also from EU or international countries abroad.

DOCUMENTS TO BE SUBMITTED FOR BA STUDY PROGRAMS

Applicants for a Bachelor study program must submit the following documents:

  • ALL documents required for the proof of the HZB (e.g. Abitur certificate or vocational training certificate (as an officially certified copy (see "the certification" on this)) and proof of professional experience in the form of an employer's reference letter (Arbeitszeugnis)).

Note: For foreign-language certificates or attestations that are not written in English, a German-language translation must be attached, the accuracy of which is certified by the competent German diplomatic or consular representation in the country of origin or by a sworn interpreter/translator in the Federal Republic of Germany.

  • Curriculum vitae in tabular form

  • Passport photo

  • Copy of the identity card or passport. The copy of the identity document for identification purposes is only permissible if all data fields, including the photograph, are blacked out with the exception of: first name, surname, date of birth and nationality.

  • In the case of previous studies / change of higher education institution: de-registration certificate (Exmatrikulationsbescheinigung) stating the reason, or certificate of good standing (Unbedenklichkeitsbescheinigung)

Note on admission to Bachelor programs with preliminary internship:

Vocational training certificates must be submitted in certified form as part of the HZB for professionally qualified applicants. If vocational training certificates only serve as a "substitute" for the proof of the preliminary internship, proof in the form of a simple copy or scan/photo is sufficient.

Please note: The student here bears the burden of providing documentation and is responsible for submitting all documents completely and correctly. Subsequent submissions of documents are only to be accepted in exceptional cases and are to be coordinated with the specialist management of Richtlinienmanagement für Immatrikulation und Anerkennung (Policy Management for Matriculation and Recognition).

Matriculation only takes place once all documents for the determination of the HZB are available.

The EAO furthermore states:

The examination board or a person commissioned by it decides at its own discretion on the acceptance of other proofs. In this case, this is the management of Richtlinienmanagement für Immatrikulation und Anerkennung.

After a positive examination of the higher education entrance qualification by the responsible clerk, written information is sent to the applicant and matriculation takes place. In the case of a negative examination of the higher education entrance qualification, the applicant is likewise informed in writing and promptly about the rejection. The documents submitted in original are returned. Copies are destroyed after a period of 3 months. The copy of the identity card is destroyed after successful identity verification.

These steps must be strictly observed and complied with!

PAGE 73 OF 245

ACCESS TO MASTER STUDIES

To be admitted to a Master study program (not MBA!), the study applicant needs:

  • A completed undergraduate study program (e.g. in the form of a Bachelor or Diplom degree) with at least 180 ECTS

  • with a focus on economics or business administration (or a focus corresponding to the study program)

  • from a state or state-recognized higher education institution / university

  • a final grade of at least "Befriedigend" (Satisfactory) (3.5) *. (see further notes in the section "Supplement on the minimum grade for admission to Master & MBA programs")

Bachelor+Master, Magister, Diplom, doctorate (Promotion) (if a successfully completed higher education degree preceded it) and Staatsexamen (state examination) also entitle to enrollment.

Here we need the certificate with the academic degree or the diploma with which the academic degree is conferred, as well as the "Transcript of Records", from which the successful completion of studies with overall grade and ECTS is evident beyond doubt.

If the acquisition of ECTS is not indicated on the certificate, the rule applies that each 6 months of standard period of study (full-time) corresponds to 30 ECTS. The certificate is then to be assessed on the basis of this rule

* - EXCEPTION:

For admission to the Master study program M.A. Architektur (120 ECTS), the undergraduate study program must have been completed with a final grade of at least "Gut" (Good) (2.5).

PAGE 74 OF 245

Supplement on the minimum grade for admission to Master & MBA programs

Applicants for Master study programs or the MBA who have a Bachelor degree that does not meet the required minimum grade of satisfactory (3.5) can, effective immediately, nevertheless be admitted if, in addition to the Bachelor degree from a recognized higher education institution, they already possess at least one third of relevant achievements at Master level (DQR 7) for the intended Master/MBA study program at the IU Internationale Hochschule and prove these. These not only compensate for the deficit of the minimum grade not achieved, but can also be credited or recognized as achievements toward the Master/MBA studies.

As additional achievements at DQR 7 level (or equivalent) for admission and crediting or recognition, the following can be taken into account:

  • A relevant completed Master/MBA from a recognized higher education institution that fits the intended Master/MBA program or
  • relevant study achievements from a Master/MBA study program at a recognized higher education institution that fit the intended Master/MBA program or
  • relevant creditable qualifications outside higher education (DQR 7) or qualifications from abroad equivalent to DQR 7, e.g. further/continuing trainings, Postgraduate Diploma programs etc., that fit the intended Master/MBA program

The following specifications and guidelines apply:

  1. Bachelor degree < 3.5 + relevant completed Master / MBA or study achievements from a Master study program / MBA from another recognized higher education institution that fit(s) the intended Master/MBA program, in the scope of at least:

    • 20 ECTS points (for Master study programs / MBA 60 ECTS points variant)
    • 30 ECTS points (for Master study programs / MBA 90 ECTS points variant)
    • 40 ECTS points (for Master study programs 120 ECTS points variant)

    Relevant higher education Master/MBA degrees and study achievements from recognized higher education institutions abroad must correspond in scope and level to the required ECTS points and can, under this condition, likewise be taken into account for admission and recognition.

  2. Bachelor degree < 3.5 + relevant creditable qualification(s) outside higher education (further/continuing trainings, Post Graduate Diploma etc. = DQR 7 or equivalent to DQR 7) that fit(s) the intended Master/MBA program, in the scope of at least:

    • 20 ECTS points (for Master study programs / MBA 60 ECTS points variant)
    • 30 ECTS points (for Master study programs / MBA 90 ECTS points variant)
    • 40 ECTS points (for Master study programs 120 ECTS points variant)

    Relevant qualifications from educational contexts abroad must correspond in scope and level to the required ECTS points and can, under this condition, likewise be taken into account for admission and crediting

PAGE 75 OF 245

Exception: For admission to the Master study program M.A. Architektur (120 ECTS), the undergraduate study program must, as before, have been completed with a final grade of at least "Gut" (Good) (2.5).

PAGE 76 OF 245

Master Zugangsprüfung (Master entrance examination)

(Contract conclusion from 01.09.2021, AGB Version 2.7)

Applicants with a Bachelor degree in a different subject area can be admitted to consecutive Master study programs of 120 ECTS by successfully taking the Master entrance examination (IU internal program, replacement for the "old" Master Interview).

The Master entrance examination has a scope of 10 ECTS and consists of 2 courses:

  • Two subject-specific courses from the intended Master study program (these are firmly defined per study program – see the admission matrix for MBA and Master study programs in the ADMISSION HUB of the RLM

The period within which the two courses must be taken and successfully passed is limited to 1 year.

The achievements of the Master entrance examination are recognized.

The applicants here are only participants and only become students after passing the Master entrance examination.

The modules of the Master entrance examination to be taken are currently defined in the OZBM.

If the applicants have already obtained 30 ECTS in the focus area of the intended study program, admission without the Master entrance examination is possible.

IMPORTANT:

  • We offer the Master entrance examination only for the 120 ECTS variant of consecutive Master programs!
  • All general admission requirements (first undergraduate higher education degree, minimum grade satisfactory /C (see supplements), + if applicable translation, English language skills, admission courses) must be fulfilled!

NOTE:

  • The Master entrance examination is valid only as a qualification for the respective study program at the IU.
  • In the event of a change of study program, the Master entrance examination may have to be taken again.

PAGE 77 OF 245

Master for Professionals - Master Eignungsprüfung (Master aptitude examination)

(Contract conclusion from 01.09.2021, AGB Version 2.7)

Master for Professionals is an admission possibility for working professionals in leadership positions who do not have a Bachelor degree to acquire access to continuing education Master study programs in the scope of 60 ECTS and 120 ECTS.

In order to obtain a Master access qualification, working professionals in leadership positions must take a Master aptitude examination (Master-Eignungsprüfung).

The Master aptitude examination consists of a written seminar paper and an oral colloquium, which is equivalent in content and scope to a Bachelor thesis.

After successfully taking the Master aptitude examination, candidates can start in all continuing education Master and MBA study programs that fit their profile in terms of subject matter.

ADMISSION CRITERIA MASTER APTITUDE EXAMINATION

The admission requirements for the Master aptitude examination (pursuant to § 70 Abs. 3 ThürHG) are:

  • completed vocational training of at least 2 years (at least DQR level 4) with subject-related reference to the intended study program,

  • subsequently at least 6 years of full-time professional practice with subject-related reference to the vocational training and to the intended study program. Of the 6 years of professional practice:

    • at least 3 years with leadership responsibility or expert activity at at least level DQR 6 must be present. Proof is provided via leadership position, managerial activity or expert activity (further training certificates, continuing training certificates, employer's reference letters, letters of reference, confirmation by the employer etc.)

As a substitute for completed vocational trainings / professional practice, the following can be accepted in accordance with the agreement with the Ministry:

  • All school-leaving qualifications at at least DQR 4 level (Abitur, Fachabitur, Fachhochschulreife)
  • Foreign school-leaving qualifications (if equivalent to Abitur, Fachabitur, Fachhochschulreife in Germany)
  • Vocational trainings not state-recognized (if equivalent to a vocational training of at least two years at at least DQR level 4, e.g. pilot training)
  • Selbstständigkeit / Self-Employment / Entrepreneurship (only as a substitute for professional practice). Proof of self-employment and leadership position is provided by a declaration in lieu of an oath (Eidesstattliche Erklärung). Additionally required: trade license (Gewerbeschein) or confirmation by a tax advisor etc.
  • Professional qualifications from EU countries abroad (if the level (EQF) assigned to the vocational training or the further training from the European country abroad is equivalent to a vocational training of at least two years at at least DQR level 4 or to the further training at at least DQR level 6)

PAGE 78 OF 245

TRANSITION FROM MASTER APTITUDE EXAMINATION TO CONTINUING EDUCATION MASTER STUDY PROGRAMS/MBA

The successfully completed Master aptitude examination enables access to all continuing education Master study programs (120 ECTS / 60 ECTS) and MBAs.

  • The Master aptitude examination is an IU internal admission program and is valid only at the IU Internationale Hochschule. The final decision lies with IU Internationale Hochschule.

  • Professional qualification and desired study program must fit together in terms of subject matter.

  • If study-program-specific subject-related prior knowledge in the form of admission courses etc. is required, these must be completed – analogously to every other applicant – before the beginning of the actual study program.

Detailed information on the admission of professionally qualified applicants to continuing education Master programs: see the guide "Master for Professionals"

PAGE 79 OF 245

Master - Professional Experience as an ECTS Bridge (Conclusion of Contract from 01.09.2021, AGB [General Terms and Conditions] Version 2.7)

Applicants at IU Internationale Hochschule with 180 ECTS from their undergraduate degree can bridge up to 60 ECTS through proof of professional experience for admission to Master's degree programs (IU internal program, replacement for TASC).

The following regulation applies here:

  • 1 year of qualified professional experience (DQR6 level) bridges 30 missing ECTS
  • 2 years of qualified professional experience (DQR6 level) bridge 60 ECTS
Bachelor ECTS Master ECTS
60 90 120
180 (60) (30) 0
210 (30) 0 0
240 0 0 0

NOTE:

ECTS bridged through professional experience do not count as official ECTS. If students wish to pursue a doctoral study program after the Master's (= 300 ECTS necessary), they may possibly not have enough ECTS to obtain the eligibility for doctoral studies.

The actually acquired number of ECTS is shown on the final degree certificate/ToR.

PAGE 80 OF 245

Master (continuing education) 120 ECTS - Early Admission Master Programme (Conclusion of Contract from 01.09.2021, AGB Version 2.7)

Applicants with a successfully completed undergraduate higher education degree, who however do not yet have the required extent of professional experience of one year in full time, can start early with a continuing education 120 ECTS Master's program within the framework of the Early Admission Master Programme.

The general admission requirements must always be fulfilled (first undergraduate higher education degree, minimum grade satisfactory /C ([see supplements]), + if applicable translation, English language skills, admission courses)!

During the Early Admission Master Programme, the persons are in participant status.

Participants can complete up to 50% of the "ECTS" of the chosen study program and in the meantime collect the still missing professional experience.

The Early Admission Master Programme is not limited in time and can extend over the entire contract term.

If the applicants can prove the missing professional experience, the transfer to the regular Master's degree program and the switch to student status take place.

NOTE:

  • Completed achievements within the framework of the Early Admission Master Programme do not yet count as official ECTS, but can only be recognized as ECTS after complete matriculation.

  • If they do not fulfill the admission requirements (proof of professional experience) by the end of the Early Admission Master Programme, they cannot continue their studies. They can indeed receive a certificate of the achievements completed, but we cannot guarantee that these will also be recognized by other universities.

PAGE 81 OF 245

English-Language Degree Programs

For English-language degree programs, proof of English language skills for non-native speakers at least at level B2 of the Common European Framework of Reference (CEFR) must be provided (the proof must be submitted BEFORE the start of studies). Accepted as language certificates are

  • TOEFL (min. 80 points, not older than 5 years)
  • IELTS (min. 6.0 out of 10 points, not older than 5 years)
  • Duolingo English Test (min. 95 points, not older than 5 years)
  • Cambridge Certificate (min. B grade overall, not older than 5 years)

For further information on official language certificates and alternative proofs of English language skills: see [Leitfaden Sprachnachweise für die Zulassung zum Studium (Guideline on Language Proofs for Admission to Studies)]

Degrees from Berufsakademien (Universities of Cooperative Education)

Degrees from Berufsakademien are in principle equivalent to a Bachelor's degree with 180 ECTS if the following note is on the diploma certificate:

"Based on the resolution of the Kultusministerkonferenz (Standing Conference of the Ministers of Education and Cultural Affairs) of 29 September 1995, the graduates of the Berufsakademien in "Bundesland" (federal state) are, in terms of professional law, to be placed on an equal footing with those of the Fachhochschulen (universities of applied sciences). Their degrees are included in the higher education diploma directives of the European Union of 21 December 1968."

Diploma certificates without this note must be checked individually by the Richtlinienmanagement für Immatrikulation und Anerkennung (Guideline Management for Matriculation and Recognition).

PAGE 82 OF 245

Special requirement for admission to the Master's distance learning program

(M.A.) Angewandtes Controlling (Applied Controlling) (MAACO-60)

In the 60 ECTS model:

  • a completed, undergraduate degree from a state or state-recognized university (of applied sciences) / university (may also have been obtained abroad — verification via Anabin or the head of the Richtlinienmanagement für Immatrikulation und Anerkennung [Guideline Management for Matriculation and Recognition]) with at least 240 ECTS. The subject area of the undergraduate degree does not matter here.

  • a final grade of at least "Satisfactory" (3.5). (see further notes in the section ["Supplement on the Minimum Grade for Admission to Master's & MBA Programs"])

  • Proof of at least one year of qualified professional experience after completion of the first undergraduate degree (internships, trainee, or working student activities can be credited after individual assessment. The proof can be submitted informally as a confirmation from the employer.)

    Professional experience from a successfully completed academically accompanied dual study program from Germany can be taken into account as proof of qualified professional experience for a continuing education Master's degree program (if the subject focus of the practical professional activity in the dual study program fits the desired Master's degree program).

Optional entry option (depending on prior education):

  • with 240 ECTS from the first degree: direct access possible

  • with 210 ECTS from the first degree: completion of courses in the amount of 30 ECTS or proof of at least one year of qualified professional experience at the beginning of the study program. (This must be acquired after completion of the undergraduate degree. The professional experience from a dual study program can not be used to bridge missing ECTS credit points.)

  • with 180 ECTS from the first degree: completion of courses in the amount of 30 ECTS and proof of at least one year of qualified professional experience at the beginning of the study program (This must be acquired after completion of the undergraduate degree.) or proof of at least two years of qualified professional experience at the beginning of the study program (This must be acquired after completion of the undergraduate degree. The professional experience from a dual study program can not be used to bridge missing ECTS credit points.)

Special prerequisite for admission to the master's distance learning program

(LL.M.) Bank- und Kapitalmarktrecht (MABKR-60)

in the 60 ECTS model:

1.) First degree program:

  • a completed undergraduate degree program in the areas of business law or business administration with at least 30 ECTS in law or any other completed law Bachelor degree program (any LL.B.) with at least 240 ECTS or a completed degree program through which the academic degree Diplom-Jurist (graduate jurist) was obtained, from a state or state-recognized university (may also be obtained abroad – verification via Anabin or the Head of Guidelines Management for Matriculation and Recognition)

  • with a final grade of at least "Befriedigend" ("Satisfactory") (3.5). (see further notes in the section "Supplement on the minimum grade for admission to Master's & MBA programs")

or

  • a completed first juridical examination pursuant to § 5 Deutsches Richtergesetz (DRiG) (German Judiciary Act) (Staatsexamen (state examination))

2.) Qualified professional experience

  • Proof of at least one year of qualified professional experience after completion of the first undergraduate degree program (internships, trainee or working-student activities can be credited after individual examination. The proof can be submitted informally as a confirmation from the employer.)

    Professional experience from a successfully completed academically accompanied dual degree program (Duales Studium) from Germany can be taken into account as proof of qualified professional experience for a continuing education master's degree program (if the subject focus of the practical professional activity in the dual degree program matches the desired master's degree program).

Optional entry option (depending on prior education):

  • with 240 ECTS from the first degree program: direct access possible

  • with 210 ECTS from the first degree program: taking courses amounting to 30 ECTS or proof of at least one year of qualified professional experience at the beginning of the study program. (This is to be acquired after completion of the undergraduate degree program.

PAGE 105 OF 245

The professional experience from a dual degree program cannot be used to bridge missing ECTS credit points.)

  • with 180 ECTS from the first degree program: taking courses amounting to 30 ECTS and proof of at least one year of qualified professional experience at the beginning of the study program (This is to be acquired after completion of the undergraduate degree program.) or proof of at least two years of qualified professional experience at the beginning of the study program (This is to be acquired after completion of the undergraduate degree program. The professional experience from a dual degree program cannot be used to bridge missing ECTS credit points.)

Additional special feature in the Master Business und IT

To be admitted to the Master in Business and IT, "proof of knowledge of a programming language" is prescribed if the Bachelor was completed in an economics degree program. The form of this proof does not matter. Possible here are: grade/ECTS in the Transcript of Records, confirmation from the employer, separate certificate obtained by taking an examination, etc. This is to be examined individually on a case-by-case basis.

See also: Fact Sheet "Programmiersprachen für den Master Wirtschaftsinformatik" ("Programming Languages for the Master in Business Informatics")

If knowledge of a programming language cannot be demonstrated, the course can be completed free of charge at the IU before the start of studies.
